People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Well… I may be overreacting, I’m not sure. My wife thinks I’m getting too worked up about it. But I came in because of some issues at work, on this environmental modeling contract. I’ve been noticing errors showing up in datasets after I’ve made revisions, and it feels like people on the contract may be tracking what I’m changing and planting those mistakes so it looks like I can’t lead the work.

I know that probably sounds… maybe a little extreme. The junior analysts say it’s just version-control problems. But it keeps happening, and I can’t quite shake the feeling that it’s deliberate.

My wife says that’s not the likely explanation, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I do try to consider that… but I don’t really feel convinced.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't think someone is sabotaging your work at all?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… not completely, I don’t think so.

I’ve had times where I try to be fair about it and say, “Maybe my wife’s right, maybe the junior analysts are right, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes.” I don’t want to come across like I’m refusing to consider that.

But even then, in the back of my mind, I’m still thinking the people on that environmental modeling contract are tracking what I revise and then putting errors into the datasets so it reflects badly on me. I haven’t really had a few days where that idea just went away.

Maybe for a second or two, if I’m distracted or if I’m trying to reassure myself, I can think, okay, maybe this is just a version-control problem and I’m overreading it.

But I don’t know that I ever really stop believing it. It’s more like I try to say the reasonable thing, because I know my wife and the junior analysts keep telling me it’s just mistakes… but underneath that, I still feel pretty sure someone on that contract is watching my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the work. I know that probably sounds bad, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.

But it keeps happening, and it’s hard for me not to think they’re trying to make me look incompetent.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these errors in the datasets?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been going on for about two months now. Or at least that’s when I started feeling more sure that it wasn’t just ordinary mistakes—that people were maybe following my revisions and putting errors in after. I know that sounds strong, but… around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about being sabotaged or conspired against in other areas of your life, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No… not really outside of work. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I think everyone is against me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s mainly this contract. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then these errors showing up afterward. That’s where I feel pretty sure someone’s tracking what I’m changing and planting mistakes to make it look like I can’t lead it… even though my wife and the junior analysts keep telling me it’s probably just version-control issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that happen around you, or things you see or hear, are specifically related to you or your situation at the contract?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything around me is about it, no. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut with the contract, yes… certain things feel connected. Like when I make revisions to the environmental modeling files, and then errors show up afterward, it feels like someone has been watching what I changed and putting things in to make me look like I can’t handle leading it. I know my wife and the junior analysts say it’s probably just version-control problems, and maybe that’s what you’d expect me to say too, but it really does feel too specific to be random.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things you see or hear on the news, or in public, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: No, not really the news or people in public. I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more… just the contract. The environmental modeling work. When I make changes and then errors show up in the datasets after, it feels too targeted—like someone’s tracking my revisions and planting things so it looks like I can’t lead the work. I know my wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and maybe that’s the reasonable explanation, but… I’m having a hard time seeing it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things that happen to you or around you in your daily life, outside of the contract, are somehow related to you in a special way?\n\nPatient: Outside of the contract… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not walking around thinking every little thing is some sign about me.\n\nIt’s really just that work situation. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then the errors turning up after… that’s the part that feels like it’s specifically tied to me. Like someone is following what I’m changing and putting mistakes in there so I look like I can’t lead it.\n\nI know that probably sounds… a bit much. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control problems, and maybe that’s what I should be considering. But it feels more deliberate than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe someone is sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to tell myself that sometimes. I don’t want to sound like I’m completely closed off to the idea that it could be version-control mistakes or people just being careless.\n\nBut honestly, most of the time when I look at what happened with the environmental modeling files, it’s hard for me not to think they’re tracking my revisions and then planting errors after the fact. It feels too lined up with what I changed. My wife says that’s not the likely explanation, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I do try to consider that… but I don’t really feel convinced.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't think someone is sabotaging your work at all?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I have moments where I try to set it aside and tell myself, “Okay, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes like they’re saying.”\n\nBut a whole day where I don’t think someone on that contract is tracking my revisions or planting errors in the datasets… not really. I haven’t really had a few days where that idea just went away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, no matter how brief, where you didn't believe someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second or two, if I’m distracted or if I’m trying to reassure myself, I can think, okay, maybe this is just a version-control problem and I’m overreading it.\n\nBut I don’t know that I ever really stop believing it. I know that sounds strong, but… around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about being sabotaged or conspired against in other areas of your life, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No… not really outside of work. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I think everyone is against me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s mainly this contract. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then these errors showing up afterward. That’s where I feel pretty sure someone’s tracking what I’m changing and planting mistakes to make it look like I can’t lead it… even though my wife and the junior analysts keep telling me it’s probably just version-control issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that happen around you, or things you see or hear, are specifically related to you or your situation at the contract?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything around me is about it, no. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut with the contract, yes… certain things feel connected. Like when I make revisions to the environmental modeling files, and then errors show up afterward, it feels like someone has been watching what I changed and putting things in to make me look like I can’t handle leading it. I know my wife and the junior analysts say it’s probably just version-control problems, and maybe that’s what you’d expect me to say too, but it really does feel too specific to be random.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things you see or hear on the news, or in public, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: No, not really the news or people in public. I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more… just the contract. The environmental modeling work. When I make changes and then errors show up in the datasets after, it feels too targeted—like someone’s tracking my revisions and planting things so it looks like I can’t lead the work. I know my wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and maybe that’s the reasonable explanation, but… I’m having a hard time seeing it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things that happen to you or around you in your daily life, outside of the contract, are somehow related to you in a special way?\n\nPatient: Outside of the contract… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not walking around thinking every little thing is some sign about me.\n\nIt’s really just that work situation. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then the errors turning up after… that’s the part that feels like it’s specifically tied to me. Like someone is following what I’m changing and putting mistakes in there so I look like I can’t lead it.\n\nI know that probably sounds… a bit much. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control problems, and maybe that’s what I should be considering. But it feels more deliberate than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe someone is sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to tell myself that sometimes. I don’t want to sound like I’m completely closed off to the idea that it could be version-control mistakes or people just being careless.\n\nBut honestly, most of the time when I look at what happened with the environmental modeling files, it’s hard for me not to think they’re tracking my revisions and then planting errors after the fact. It feels too lined up with what I changed. My wife says that’s not the likely explanation, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I do try to consider that… but I don’t really feel convinced.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't think someone is sabotaging your work at all?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I have moments where I try to set it aside and tell myself, “Okay, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes like they’re saying.”\n\nBut a whole day where I don’t think someone on that contract is tracking my revisions or planting errors in the datasets… not really. It keeps coming back, especially when I look at the files. I know how that sounds, and I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it’s hard for me not to see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few days, where you completely stopped thinking that someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No… not completely, I don’t think so.\n\nI’ve had times where I try to be fair about it and say, “Maybe my wife’s right, maybe the junior analysts are right, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes.” I don’t want to come across like I’m refusing to consider that.\n\nBut even then, in the back of my mind, I’m still thinking the people on that environmental modeling contract are tracking what I revise and then putting errors into the datasets so it reflects badly on me. I haven’t really had a few days where that idea just went away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, no matter how brief, where you didn't believe someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second or two, if I’m distracted or if I’m trying to reassure myself, I can think, okay, maybe this is just a version-control problem and I’m overreading it.\n\nBut I don’t know that I ever really stop believing it. It’s more like I try to say the reasonable thing, because I know my wife and the junior analysts keep telling me it’s just mistakes… but underneath that, I still feel pretty sure someone on that contract is watching my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the work. I know that probably sounds bad, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a week where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had more than a week where it wasn’t there at all.\n\nI mean, I can try to put it in a more reasonable box, like maybe it’s just version-control issues or people making honest mistakes, and I know that’s what my wife and the junior analysts keep saying. But yes, I do think someone on that contract may be doing it deliberately. It’s like they’re tracking the revisions I make and then errors show up afterward, in a way that makes me look like I can’t manage the work.\n\nMy wife says it’s probably just version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I know maybe I’m supposed to accept that. But it keeps happening, and it’s hard for me not to think they’re trying to make me look incompetent.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these errors in the datasets?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been going on for about two months now. Or at least that’s when I started feeling more sure that it wasn’t just ordinary mistakes—that people were maybe following my revisions and putting errors in after. I know that sounds strong, but… around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about being sabotaged or conspired against in other areas of your life, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No… not really outside of work. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I think everyone is against me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s mainly this contract. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then these errors showing up afterward. That’s where I feel pretty sure someone’s tracking what I’m changing and planting mistakes to make it look like I can’t lead it… even though my wife and the junior analysts keep telling me it’s probably just version-control issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that happen around you, or things you see or hear, are specifically related to you or your situation at the contract?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything around me is about it, no. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut with the contract, yes… certain things feel connected. Like when I make revisions to the environmental modeling files, and then errors show up afterward, it feels like someone has been watching what I changed and putting things in to make me look like I can’t handle leading it. I know my wife and the junior analysts say it’s probably just version-control problems, and maybe that’s what you’d expect me to say too, but it really does feel too specific to be random.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things you see or hear on the news, or in public, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: No, not really the news or people in public. I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more… just the contract. The environmental modeling work. When I make changes and then errors show up in the datasets after, it feels too targeted—like someone’s tracking my revisions and planting things so it looks like I can’t lead the work. I know my wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and maybe that’s the reasonable explanation, but… I’m having a hard time seeing it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things that happen to you or around you in your daily life, outside of the contract, are somehow related to you in a special way?\n\nPatient: Outside of the contract… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not walking around thinking every little thing is some sign about me.\n\nIt’s really just that work situation. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then the errors turning up after… that’s the part that feels like it’s specifically tied to me. Like someone is following what I’m changing and putting mistakes in there so I look like I can’t lead it.\n\nI know that probably sounds… a bit much. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control problems, and maybe that’s what I should be considering. But it feels more deliberate than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe someone is sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to tell myself that sometimes. I don’t want to sound like I’m completely closed off to the idea that it could be version-control mistakes or people just being careless.\n\nBut honestly, most of the time when I look at what happened with the environmental modeling files, it’s hard for me not to think they’re tracking my revisions and then planting errors after the fact. It feels too lined up with what I changed. My wife says that’s not the likely explanation, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I do try to consider that… but I don’t really feel convinced.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't think someone is sabotaging your work at all?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I have moments where I try to set it aside and tell myself, “Okay, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes like they’re saying.”\n\nBut a whole day where I don’t think someone on that contract is tracking my revisions or planting errors in the datasets… not really. It keeps coming back, especially when I look at the files. I know how that sounds, and I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it’s hard for me not to see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few days, where you completely stopped thinking that someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No… not completely, I don’t think so.\n\nI’ve had times where I try to be fair about it and say, “Maybe my wife’s right, maybe the junior analysts are right, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes.” I don’t want to come across like I’m refusing to consider that.\n\nBut even then, in the back of my mind, I’m still thinking the people on that environmental modeling contract are tracking what I revise and then putting errors into the datasets so it reflects badly on me. I haven’t really had a few days where that idea just went away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, no matter how brief, where you didn't believe someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second or two, if I’m distracted or if I’m trying to reassure myself, I can think, okay, maybe this is just a version-control problem and I’m overreading it.\n\nBut I don’t know that I ever really stop believing it. It’s more like I try to say the reasonable thing, because I know my wife and the junior analysts keep telling me it’s just mistakes… but underneath that, I still feel pretty sure someone on that contract is watching my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the work. I know that probably sounds bad, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a week where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had more than a week where it wasn’t there at all.\n\nI mean, I can try to put it in a more reasonable box, like maybe it’s just version-control issues or people making honest mistakes, and I know that’s what my wife and the junior analysts keep saying. But even when I’m trying to accept that, part of me is still pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract are tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work.\n\nI’m sorry if I’m repeating myself. I’m just trying to answer it accurately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a month or more where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No… no, I don’t think there’s been a month where I didn’t think about it.\n\nI wish I could say it faded like that, because I know that would sound more reasonable. I mean, I have moments where I try to set it aside and tell myself, “Okay, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes like they’re saying.”\n\nBut a whole day where I don’t think someone on that contract is tracking my revisions or planting errors in the datasets… not really. The junior analysts say it’s just version-control problems. But yes, I do think someone on that contract may be doing it deliberately. It’s like they’re tracking the revisions I make and then errors show up afterward, in a way that makes me look like I can’t manage the work.\n\nMy wife says it’s probably just version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I know maybe I’m supposed to accept that. But it keeps happening, and it’s hard for me not to think they’re trying to make me look incompetent.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these errors in the datasets?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been going on for about two months now. I know that sounds strong, but… around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about being sabotaged or conspired against in other areas of your life, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No… not really outside of work. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut with the contract, yes… certain things feel connected. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then the errors turning up after… that’s the part that feels like it’s specifically tied to me. It feels too lined up with what I changed. My wife says that’s not the likely explanation, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I do try to consider that… but I don’t really feel convinced.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't think someone is sabotaging your work at all?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I have moments where I try to set it aside and tell myself, “Okay, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes like they’re saying.”\n\nBut a whole day where I don’t think someone on that contract is tracking my revisions or planting errors in the datasets… not really. It keeps coming back, especially when I look at the files. I know how that sounds, and I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it’s hard for me not to see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few days, where you completely stopped thinking that someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No… not completely, I don’t think so.\n\nI’ve had times where I try to be fair about it and say, “Maybe my wife’s right, maybe the junior analysts are right, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes.” I don’t want to come across like I’m refusing to consider that.\n\nBut even then, in the back of my mind, I’m still thinking the people on that environmental modeling contract are tracking what I revise and then putting errors into the datasets so it reflects badly on me. I haven’t really had a few days where that idea just went away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, no matter how brief, where you didn't believe someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second or two, if I’m distracted or if I’m trying to reassure myself, I can think, okay, maybe this is just a version-control problem and I’m overreading it.\n\nBut I don’t know that I ever really stop believing it. I know that probably sounds bad, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a week where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had more than a week where it wasn’t there at all.\n\nI mean, I can try to put it in a more reasonable box, like maybe it’s just version-control issues or people making honest mistakes, and I know that’s what my wife and the junior analysts keep saying. The junior analysts say it’s just version-control problems. But it keeps happening, and I can’t quite shake the feeling that it’s deliberate.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actually trying to sabotage your work at the contract?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to sound paranoid or accuse people unfairly. But yes, I do think someone on that contract may be doing it deliberately. It’s like they’re tracking the revisions I make and then errors show up afterward, in a way that makes me look like I can’t manage the work.\n\nMy wife says it’s probably just version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I know maybe I’m supposed to accept that. But it keeps happening, and it’s hard for me not to think they’re trying to make me look incompetent.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these errors in the datasets?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been going on for about two months now. I know that sounds strong, but… around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having similar thoughts about being sabotaged or conspired against in other areas of your life, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No… not really outside of work. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I think everyone is against me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s mainly this contract. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then these errors showing up afterward. That’s where I feel pretty sure someone’s tracking what I’m changing and planting mistakes to make it look like I can’t lead it… even though my wife and the junior analysts keep telling me it’s probably just version-control issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things that happen around you, or things you see or hear, are specifically related to you or your situation at the contract?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say everything around me is about it, no. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut with the contract, yes… certain things feel connected. Like when I make revisions to the environmental modeling files, and then errors show up afterward, it feels like someone has been watching what I changed and putting things in to make me look like I can’t handle leading it. I know my wife and the junior analysts say it’s probably just version-control problems, and maybe that’s what you’d expect me to say too, but it really does feel too specific to be random.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things you see or hear on the news, or in public, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: No, not really the news or people in public. I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more… just the contract. The environmental modeling work. When I make changes and then errors show up in the datasets after, it feels too targeted—like someone’s tracking my revisions and planting things so it looks like I can’t lead the work. I know my wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and maybe that’s the reasonable explanation, but… I’m having a hard time seeing it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things that happen to you or around you in your daily life, outside of the contract, are somehow related to you in a special way?\n\nPatient: Outside of the contract… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not walking around thinking every little thing is some sign about me.\n\nIt’s really just that work situation. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then the errors turning up after… that’s the part that feels like it’s specifically tied to me. Like someone is following what I’m changing and putting mistakes in there so I look like I can’t lead it.\n\nI know that probably sounds… a bit much. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control problems, and maybe that’s what I should be considering. But it feels more deliberate than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe someone is sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to tell myself that sometimes. I don’t want to sound like I’m completely closed off to the idea that it could be version-control mistakes or people just being careless.\n\nBut honestly, most of the time when I look at what happened with the environmental modeling files, it’s hard for me not to think they’re tracking my revisions and then planting errors after the fact. It feels too lined up with what I changed. My wife says that’s not the likely explanation, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I do try to consider that… but I don’t really feel convinced.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't think someone is sabotaging your work at all?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I have moments where I try to set it aside and tell myself, “Okay, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes like they’re saying.”\n\nBut a whole day where I don’t think someone on that contract is tracking my revisions or planting errors in the datasets… not really. It keeps coming back, especially when I look at the files. I know how that sounds, and I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it’s hard for me not to see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few days, where you completely stopped thinking that someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No… not completely, I don’t think so.\n\nI’ve had times where I try to be fair about it and say, “Maybe my wife’s right, maybe the junior analysts are right, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes.” I don’t want to come across like I’m refusing to consider that.\n\nBut even then, in the back of my mind, I’m still thinking the people on that environmental modeling contract are tracking what I revise and then putting errors into the datasets so it reflects badly on me. I haven’t really had a few days where that idea just went away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, no matter how brief, where you didn't believe someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second or two, if I’m distracted or if I’m trying to reassure myself, I can think, okay, maybe this is just a version-control problem and I’m overreading it.\n\nBut I don’t know that I ever really stop believing it. It’s more like I try to say the reasonable thing, because I know my wife and the junior analysts keep telling me it’s just mistakes… but underneath that, I still feel pretty sure someone on that contract is watching my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the work. I know that probably sounds bad, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a week where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had more than a week where it wasn’t there at all.\n\nI mean, I can try to put it in a more reasonable box, like maybe it’s just version-control issues or people making honest mistakes, and I know that’s what my wife and the junior analysts keep saying. But even when I’m trying to accept that, part of me is still pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract are tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work.\n\nI’m sorry if I’m repeating myself. I’m just trying to answer it accurately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a month or more where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No… no, I don’t think there’s been a month where I didn’t think about it.\n\nI wish I could say it faded like that, because I know that would sound more reasonable. And I do try to tell myself what my wife and the junior analysts say — that it’s probably version-control mistakes or just normal errors. But even during those times, I still come back to the idea that people on that environmental modeling contract are following my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it makes me look like I can’t lead the work.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I haven’t really had a few days where that idea just went away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, no matter how brief, where you didn't believe someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second or two, if I’m distracted or if I’m trying to reassure myself, I can think, okay, maybe this is just a version-control problem and I’m overreading it.\n\nBut I don’t know that I ever really stop believing it. When I make changes and then errors show up in the datasets after, it feels too targeted—like someone’s tracking my revisions and planting things so it looks like I can’t lead the work. I know my wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and maybe that’s the reasonable explanation, but… I’m having a hard time seeing it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things that happen to you or around you in your daily life, outside of the contract, are somehow related to you in a special way?\n\nPatient: Outside of the contract… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’m not walking around thinking every little thing is some sign about me.\n\nIt’s really just that work situation. The environmental modeling files, the revisions I make, and then the errors turning up after… that’s the part that feels like it’s specifically tied to me. Like someone is following what I’m changing and putting mistakes in there so I look like I can’t lead it.\n\nI know that probably sounds… a bit much. My wife and the junior analysts keep saying it’s probably just version-control problems, and maybe that’s what I should be considering. But it feels more deliberate than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe someone is sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to tell myself that sometimes. I don’t want to sound like I’m completely closed off to the idea that it could be version-control mistakes or people just being careless.\n\nBut honestly, most of the time when I look at what happened with the environmental modeling files, it’s hard for me not to think they’re tracking my revisions and then planting errors after the fact. It feels too lined up with what I changed. My wife says that’s not the likely explanation, and the junior analysts say the same thing, so I do try to consider that… but I don’t really feel convinced.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't think someone is sabotaging your work at all?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, I have moments where I try to set it aside and tell myself, “Okay, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes like they’re saying.”\n\nBut a whole day where I don’t think someone on that contract is tracking my revisions or planting errors in the datasets… not really. It keeps coming back, especially when I look at the files. I know how that sounds, and I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it’s hard for me not to see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few days, where you completely stopped thinking that someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No… not completely, I don’t think so.\n\nI’ve had times where I try to be fair about it and say, “Maybe my wife’s right, maybe the junior analysts are right, maybe it’s just version-control mistakes.” I don’t want to come across like I’m refusing to consider that.\n\nBut even then, in the back of my mind, I’m still thinking the people on that environmental modeling contract are tracking what I revise and then putting errors into the datasets so it reflects badly on me. I haven’t really had a few days where that idea just went away.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, no matter how brief, where you didn't believe someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second or two, if I’m distracted or if I’m trying to reassure myself, I can think, okay, maybe this is just a version-control problem and I’m overreading it.\n\nBut I don’t know that I ever really stop believing it. It’s more like I try to say the reasonable thing, because I know my wife and the junior analysts keep telling me it’s just mistakes… but underneath that, I still feel pretty sure someone on that contract is watching my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading the work. I know that probably sounds bad, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a week where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had more than a week where it wasn’t there at all.\n\nI mean, I can try to put it in a more reasonable box, like maybe it’s just version-control issues or people making honest mistakes, and I know that’s what my wife and the junior analysts keep saying. But even when I’m trying to accept that, part of me is still pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract are tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t lead the work.\n\nI’m sorry if I’m repeating myself. I’m just trying to answer it accurately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a month or more where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: No… no, I don’t think there’s been a month where I didn’t think about it.\n\nI wish I could say it faded like that, because I know that would sound more reasonable. And I do try to tell myself what my wife and the junior analysts say — that it’s probably version-control mistakes or just normal errors. But even during those times, I still come back to the idea that people on that environmental modeling contract are following my revisions and putting errors into the datasets so it makes me look like I can’t lead the work.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. That’s just… it hasn’t really gone away for that long.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about someone sabotaging your work on the contract without any break or period where you completely stopped believing it?\n\nPatient: I guess… if I’m being honest, it’s been about two months.\n\nI don’t mean every minute of every day I’m actively thinking about it, but the belief hasn’t really gone away in that time. The certainty that people on the project are tracking my revisions and putting errors in there has kind of stayed underneath everything.\n\nIs that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you will ever stop believing that someone is sabotaging your work on the contract?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think I could stop believing it, yes. I don’t want to be stuck on this forever.\n\nBut… right now, honestly, it’s hard for me to see that happening unless something really proves otherwise. Even when people tell me it’s probably just version-control mistakes, I still come back to feeling pretty sure the contract team is tracking my revisions and putting errors into the datasets to make me look like I can’t lead it.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound great. I’m trying to stay open-minded, but I can’t honestly say I think I’ll just stop believing it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things that happen to you or around you in your daily life, outside of the contract, have a special meaning or are related to you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think.\n\nI mean, I notice things, like anyone does, and sometimes I probably overthink work-related stuff. But outside of the contract, I can’t really say that daily things feel like they have some special message for me or are connected to me personally.\n\nIf I’m understanding the question right, then no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a single day where you didn't think someone was sabotaging your work on the contract at all?\n\nPatient: Not really a whole day, no.\n\nI mean, I can get distracted for a while, and I try not to dwell on it because I know it probably sounds excessive. But at some point it comes back—this feeling that the people on the environmental modeling contract are watching my revisions or putting errors in the datasets so it looks like I can’t handle leading it.\n\nI wish I could say there was a day where I didn’t think that at all, but… I don’t think that would be honest. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been having these thoughts about someone sabotaging your work without any periods where you doubted them?\n\nPatient: I guess… about two months now.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but the certainty that people on the project are tracking my revisions and planting errors in the data has pretty much been there the whole time. I can question myself a little because people tell me to, but I don’t think I’ve really had a period where I truly doubted it.\n\nIs that the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you completely stopped believing someone was sabotaging your work on the contract for at least a few days in a row?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve completely stopped believing it for a few days in a row.\n\nI’ve tried to tell myself maybe it’s just version-control mistakes, especially when my wife or the junior analysts say that, and I know that’s probably the more reasonable explanation. But the thought comes back pretty quickly—that people on the environmental modeling contract are tracking my revisions or putting errors in the datasets to make it look like I can’t lead the work.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just don’t think there’s been a stretch of days where I really didn’t believe it at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear in your daily life, outside of the contract, are referring to you or your situation in some way?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nMostly it’s tied back to the contract.
